# Plan-review handoff: f-20260921-05 — one identity for a pre-id engine across the three engine owner keys

Load this file before reviewing a successor of `f-20260921-05` or the diff that implements its
plan. Inherited issue IDs: I1–I9 (I1, I3, I5, I6 closed in plan review; I2 carried to the cumulative
diff review as CR-1, together with I8's case (f); I7 and I9 carried as CR-2; I4 deferred as a filed
finding). Plan: `tasks/plans/2026-10-07-legacy-engine-identity.md` (gitignored). Planned ahead in
drain run `0ab86bf9-7e6e-42ce-b073-c494cb240f4f` by PLAN-ONLY session `e5ac80e2-4c45-4d5f-b424-73ead0be75b8` at base
`932d290c59baeccbe5b9c3c1c95f3c236aee95b6`. Refreshed in drain session `464febf7-d016-4983-8c85-577ec2d48026` at

Executor Codex (OpenAI high tier) ran every lens and both focused judgments; Claude orchestrated and
arbitrated the original rounds, and Grok arbitrated the refresh — the same context that wrote each
plan revision arbitrated its triage.

**The diff review must close CR-1 and CR-2.** CR-1 is the I2 lineage: plan review ended on it under
rule 12a after the second focused judgment (`JUDGMENT: carry`); it is open, not closed. Its
counterexamples (a)–(f) and every I2 case recorded below must each be proven by a test over the
real implementation. CR-2 includes I9: `EnginesPage.removal.test.tsx:124` must expect the null
assessment field while its removal assertions stay.

## Mandate (fixed)

Close `f-20260921-05`: `engines`, `game-player1-settings` and `game-player2-settings` are
hydrated and migrated independently, and a pre-id engine record's id is minted by the schema's
`crypto.randomUUID()` default, so the same legacy engine gets one id under `engines` and a different
one under each player key; since f-20260906-15 validates the selection by id, the saved game-player
selection is replaced by the first remaining engine (picker) or refused (submission). Open
question: a cross-key map built in one coordinated migration, or an id derived deterministically
from the legacy fields.

## Planned answer

- **O1:** a pre-id local record whose handle + name is unique in the `engines` list gets the
  identity id `D(handle.id, name)` (bounded synchronous digest, no dependency); ambiguous groups and
  remote records get positional ids from a disjoint namespace; the duplicate-id repair becomes
  deterministic; `enginesSchema` moves to `engineOwnerStorage.ts` (re-exported from `atoms.ts`).
- **O2:** a pre-id player snapshot resolves to (1) id `D` of its own handle + name, (2) the one
  record with a non-ambiguous legacy claim for `D`, (3) before any assessment, the same over an
  in-memory assessment, else (4) `D` unresolved; untrusted `engines` bytes withhold the player
  migration write.
- **O4:** random-era local records (ids minted by the defective migration) are assessed once, from
  the `engines` bytes alone, at the first trusted `engines` hydration: claim of their own handle +
  name, ambiguous over the complete group; `null` otherwise; every `enginesAtom` write keeps the
  field for surviving local ids and sets `null` on new local ids; remote records never carry it.
- **O3:** unpersisted migrations yield the same ids and claims on every hydration of the same bytes.
- Out of scope: repairing player snapshots that already carry an absent id; the element-level random
  defaults; startup ownership evidence; the picker's own absent-engine behaviour (I4, filed).

## Carried to diff review

* **CR-1 (I2, carried by the r4 focused `review-plan` judgment under rule 12a; open, not closed by
  plan review):** a pre-id player selection whose legacy identity was ambiguous, or whose engine no
  longer exists, never becomes resolvable to a different `engines` entry under ordinary UI
  operations and write failures, across restarts. The cumulative diff review closes it only with
  tests proving every counterexample recorded under I2 in this plan's `## Reviews` (committed by the
  adopting session as `tasks/handoffs/2026-10-07-f-20260921-05-review.md`), at least: (a) pre-id twins A/B with positional ids, a failed player write, A duplicated to C
  and C renamed to the shared name, restart → A's selection unresolved; (b) random-era A/B sharing
  handle and name with a duplicate random id repaired by O1 → a snapshot of B unresolved; (c)
  random-era A/B, B duplicated to C, A and B removed, C renamed to the shared name, restart →
  unresolved; (d) the `engines` hydration write fails, A removed through a successful `enginesAtom`
  write, restart → B's persisted ambiguous claim keeps A's selection unresolved; (e) a failed
  `engines` migration followed by a successful `engines` write persists the assessment; (f) a player
  key whose read throws during the first trusted `engines` hydration does not skip or change the
  assessment (random-era twins A/B, A removed, restart with readable keys → A's selection
  unresolved).
* **CR-2 (I7, extended by I9):** existing tests that seed or add local engines through `enginesAtom` and assert
  exact equality (`src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.test.tsx:217`, `:270`, `:383`,
  `src/components/engines/EnginesPage.removal.test.tsx:124` after the real-atom seed at `:91`, and any
  other such test the proof run reports) update their fixtures or expected values for the `null`
  field the write path now sets on new local ids, keeping every behavioural assertion. The removal
  test keeps its order, retirement, storage and `MissingLocalEngineError` assertions.

**Focused fresh-context judgment (rule 12a; artefact `judge-i1-r3`, review-plan role, Codex).** Contested
invariant: a pre-id player selection of a random-era record resolves to that record's id `R` even if
the player write fails and a later `engines` write changes its handle/name or removes a same-name
sibling. Previous answers: r1 current-list match; r2 + O4 settle; r3 digest `D` + random-era fallback
with a named limit. New evidence given: every owner write is prepare → setItem → reconcile
(`engineOwnerStorage.ts:166-234`); the removal flow ignores the receipt and `enginesAtom` publishes
even on a failed save (`atoms.ts:265-270`, open f-20261001-17). Options judged: (A) drop random-era
recovery, (B) re-key `R` to `D`, (F) refuse `engines` writes until player writes land, (L) keep the
limit. Judgment (verbatim last line): `JUDGMENT: other: immutable engines-owned legacy-resolution
evidence`; random-era data is within the MANDATE; A drops required recovery, B changes `R` and needs
another dependent-owner migration, F cannot hold publication and retirement behaviour without
expanding into f-20261001-17, L retains I1/I2. Resulting obligation changes adopted as the r4
correction above; the orchestrator added one choice within it: claim ownership is enforced once, in
the `enginesAtom` write path, instead of at each creating or copying call site (duplicate, addition,
JSON editor, catalog install).

**Second focused fresh-context judgment (rule 12a; artefact `judge-i2-r4`, review-plan role, Codex).**
Contested invariant: an ambiguous or removed pre-id selection never becomes resolvable to another
entry under ordinary UI operations and write failures. Input: the three r4 counterexamples, the
error-handling should-fix, and the orchestrator's proposal (tri-state field, assess once, complete
group, `null` for new ids, skip assessment when a player read fails). Judgment: the proposal closes
(a)-(c), but its read-failure skip reopens I2 (twins A/B, a player read throws, A removed through a
successful write — `engineOwnerStorage.ts:209-215` writes before returning `synchronized: false` —
then B is the unique unassessed candidate after restart); the simplest correction is an
unconditional one-time assessment from trusted `engines` bytes without reading player keys; and plan
review ends here with I2 carried as an open implementation obligation whose diff closure must prove
(a)-(c), the read-refusal/removal/restart case, and a failed migration followed by a successful
`engines` write. Verbatim last line: `JUDGMENT: carry`. Adopted: O4/O2/test 7 rewritten to the
judged mechanism, CR-1 added; one closure round (r5) follows only because the plan text changed.

  {
    "claim": "With engines unreadable or invalid, the mounted game-player picker replaces the saved selection with null and the player owner persists it; pre-existing for every selection.",
    "dependencies": [],
    "disposition": "Defer",
    "entry": "### An unreadable or invalid engines list makes the game-player picker discard the saved engine selection\n\n* **ID:** f-PENDING · **Status:** open · **Area:** frontend-state · **Root:** - · **Entry:** build · **Blocked:** none\n* **Filed from:** e5ac80e2-4c45-4d5f-b424-73ead0be75b8\n* **Where:** `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts` (`createEngineOwnerStorage` → `getItem` returns `initialValue` for an unreadable, undecodable or rejected `engines` value), `src/state/atoms.ts` (`enginesAtom` then exposes `[]`), `src/components/boards/EnginesSelect.tsx:31-37` (an id absent from the list is replaced by the first remaining engine or `null`), `src/components/boards/OpponentForm.tsx:78-92` (the replacement also clears per-game engine settings), `src/state/atoms.ts:569-572` (the player owner persists the replacement through the ordinary save route, which writes even when a sibling owner is untrusted, `engineOwnerStorage.ts:209-215`).\n* **Defect:** when the `engines` key cannot be read or hydrated, the engines owner falls back to the same empty list that means \"no engines installed\". Opening game setup mounts `EnginesSelect`, which treats that fallback as \"the selected engine was removed\" and replaces the saved selection with `null`, and the player owner persists the replacement. The saved selection and its per-game settings are lost even though the engine still exists in the unreadable bytes, so a later launch that can read `engines` again has nothing left to resolve. The behaviour is independent of legacy-id migration: it happens for any saved selection whenever the engines owner falls back.\n* **Open question:** how does a consumer tell \"the engines list could not be loaded\" apart from \"the engines list is empty\" — an explicit hydration-failure state on the engines owner that the picker and submission respect (no automatic replacement write while it holds), or a player-owner write guard that refuses replacement writes while the sibling `engines` owner is untrusted?\n* **Why it matters:** `.claude/rules/persisted-state.md` requires a persisted value naming something contextual to be corrected with a derived effective value rather than a destructive reset; here a read failure of one key destroys durable data in another.\n* **Related:** f-20260921-05 (its plan review, round 1, error-handling and persisted-state lenses, raised this against that plan's \"withhold the player migration write while `engines` is untrusted\" clause; the destruction is pre-existing and not caused by legacy ids), f-20260906-15 (handled; added the id-based picker reconciliation).\n* **Found by:** f-20260921-05 PLAN-ONLY plan review round 1 (`review-error-handling`, `review-persisted-state`), 2026-10-07; the orchestrator confirmed `EnginesSelect.tsx:31-37` and `engineOwnerStorage.ts:404-410` before filing.\n",
    "id": "I4",
    "reason": "Pre-existing hazard independent of legacy ids (EnginesSelect.tsx:31-37, engineOwnerStorage.ts:404-410); a filed finding, not a new mechanism in this plan (build §4).",

### lens-correctness-r1.txt

### plan-r1.md

# Plan: one identity for a pre-id engine across the three engine owner keys (f-20260921-05)

## Goal

A pre-id engine record keeps naming the same engine after migration: the `engines` list and both
game-player owner keys end up carrying the same id for it, whatever order the three keys hydrate in,
whether or not a migration write lands, and across restarts — so the game-player picker and game
submission (which resolve by id since f-20260906-15) find the engine the saved selection names.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20260921-05:

* **Where:** `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts:314-370` (`isLegacyIdentityMigration`, `parseHydratableOwner`), `src/utils/engines.ts:98` (`id: z.string().default(() => crypto.randomUUID())`), `src/state/atoms.ts:194-200` and `480-497` (the three owner keys).
* **Defect:** `engines`, `game-player1-settings` and `game-player2-settings` are hydrated and migrated independently, and the id for a pre-id engine record is minted by the schema's `crypto.randomUUID()` default. The same legacy engine therefore receives one id under `engines` and a different one under each player key. Since f-20260906-15 the selection is validated by id, so after such a migration the saved game-player selection is silently replaced by the first remaining engine (picker) or refused (submission) rather than resolving to the engine it names. Before that validation the mismatch was invisible but equally wrong: the player config carried an id the supervisor did not know.
* **Fix direction:** migration has to reconcile identity across the three owner keys — mint ids once for the `engines` owner and resolve the player owners' legacy records against it by the fields `compareLegacyEngineFields` already compares, rather than defaulting each key independently. The coordinator in d-20260913-01 already serializes the three keys, so the ordering exists; what is missing is a shared identity map. Deriving a stable id from the legacy fields is the alternative and changes what an id means.
* **Open question:** is the pre-id identity resolved by a cross-key map built during one coordinated migration, or by making the minted id a deterministic function of the legacy engine fields? The first keeps ids opaque but needs the three keys migrated in one transaction with a crash boundary; the second makes migration order irrelevant but ties the id to fields a user can edit.
* **Review lens:** persisted-state.

## Threat model and non-goals

Accidental input only: owner values written by earlier versions of this app (records without an
`id`), records that drifted apart after selection (a player snapshot is a copy taken at selection
time; the `engines` record is later edited, toggled or upgraded), migration writes that fail
(quota, native prepare failure) or lose a raw-value conflict, process exit between the writes of
different keys, and two windows of the same app version hydrating concurrently. Out of scope:
deliberately hand-edited `localStorage`, and downgrading to a pre-id app version that writes
id-less records again after migration.

## Traced premises

* `src/utils/engines.ts:111` and `:231` — `localEngineSchema` / `remoteEngineSchema` default a missing `id` to `crypto.randomUUID()`; every parse of the same id-less bytes mints a different id.
* `src/state/atoms.ts:221-230` — `enginesSchema` re-mints a duplicate id with `crypto.randomUUID()`; `:233-237` the `engines` owner hydrates through `createEngineOwnerStorage("engines", enginesSchema, [])`.
* `src/state/atoms.ts:556-569` — each player owner hydrates through `createEngineOwnerStorage(key, opponentSettingsSchema, defaultPlayerSettings)`; `src/state/opponentSettings.ts:52` its `engine` is a full `localEngineSchema` snapshot, so its id is minted by the same per-parse default.
* `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts:374-384` `parseHydratableOwner` parses each key alone; `:322-372` `isLegacyIdentityMigration` accepts an id-only difference between raw and parsed; `:391-425` `getItem` persists the migration through `migrateEngineOwnerValue` (`:243`, raw-value conflict guard at `:200`), and on a non-conflict failure (`:422`) or the second conflict (`:420`, `allowMigrationRetry=false`) returns the freshly minted value **without persisting it**, so the next launch mints yet another id.
* Probe 2 (`probe-2-r1`): all three owner atoms omit `getOnInit` and hydrate on first subscription; `BoardGame.tsx:167-168` is the only reader of the player owners, while `engines` is subscribed by many screens — there is no hydration order between the keys, and a player key can hydrate in a later session than `engines`.
* Probe 1 (`probe-1-r1`): `src/components/boards/EnginesSelect.tsx:31-37` replaces a selection whose id is absent from the `engines` list with the first remaining local engine (persisted through the player owner); `src/components/boards/playerConfig.ts:39` refuses submission for an absent id; `EnginesSelect.tsx:38-40` refreshes a same-id snapshot from the live record, so a snapshot diverging from its `engines` record is normal (`loaded`/`enabled`/settings/name edits only change the `engines` record); `EnginesSelect.tsx:53` stores the complete `engines` record (with its id) on selection.
* The serialization the finding attributes to d-20260913-01 is in fact the module-level `sequence` chain in `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts:158-234`; d-20260913-01 is the database A2 operation matrix and does not govern this cluster.
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:919`, `:1939` — native engine ids are plain `String`s; `retire_engine` tombstones an id for the process lifetime (d-20260901-17). No production schema validates an engine id's shape (`git grep '\.uuid()' src` hits only tab and workspace ids).

## Approach

The open question is decided as a hybrid that needs no cross-key transaction: **every identity a
migration assigns is a pure function of the stored bytes**, and a player key's pre-id snapshot is
**resolved against the `engines` list** rather than minted. Because both sides are deterministic,
hydration order, a failed write, a crash between two keys' writes, and two concurrent windows all
yield the same ids; no coordinator, lock or transaction is added.

### O1 — Deterministic identity for the `engines` list

Rests on: "the id for a pre-id engine record is minted by the schema's `crypto.randomUUID()`
default"; "mint ids once for the `engines` owner".

* Hydrating the `engines` key assigns every record that has no string `id` an id computed only from
  the decoded list: the same decoded bytes always yield the same ids. Ids are unique within the list
  and never equal any id already present in the raw list. The derivation does not mutate the decoded
  input (the raw-vs-parsed comparison in `isLegacyIdentityMigration` depends on it).
* The duplicate-id repair in `enginesSchema` becomes deterministic the same way (rule 4b, same file
  and same defect class: a re-minted id that is not persisted changes on every launch, so a player
  selection made on the repaired record in that session no longer names it after restart).
* The derived id is an opaque string; its exact format is the executor's choice. Once persisted it is
  never re-derived, so later edits to the record's fields do not change it.
* `enginesSchema` moves out of `src/state/atoms.ts` into `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts` (O2 needs
  it there; `atoms.ts` already imports that module, the reverse would be circular). `atoms.ts` keeps
  exporting it so existing imports stay valid.
* The strict startup inspection (`schemas.engines`, `inspectEngineOwnerValue`,
  `collectOriginalEngineOwnerSnapshot`) is unchanged.

### O2 — A player key's pre-id snapshot resolves to the `engines` record it names

Rests on: "resolve the player owners' legacy records against it"; "rather than resolving to the
engine it names".

* When a player owner (`game-player1-settings` / `game-player2-settings`) hydrates an `engine`
  branch whose `engine` has no string `id`, its id is taken from the `engines` list as O1 would
  hydrate it from the bytes currently stored under `engines` (already-persisted ids, or O1-derived
  ids for a list that is itself still pre-id). It is never minted independently.
* Match order over the list's local engines, first match in list order within the best non-empty
  tier: (1) equal on every field except `id` (`compareLegacyEngineFields`); (2) same `handle.id` and
  same `name`; (3) same `handle.id`. Tiers 2-3 exist because a snapshot legitimately diverges from
  its record (premise: `EnginesSelect.tsx:38-40`).
* No match (the engine was removed, or the `engines` key is absent): the snapshot gets a
  deterministic id that is not present in the `engines` list, so the existing picker/submission
  "absent engine" handling applies unchanged and stays stable across launches.
* Failure semantics: if the `engines` bytes cannot be read, decoded, or hydrated (the same
  conditions under which the `engines` owner itself falls back to its initial value), the resolution
  has no trusted evidence — the player value is returned for display but its migration is **not
  persisted**, so a later launch with readable `engines` bytes can still resolve it.
* The resolved id is persisted through the existing `migrateEngineOwnerValue` route (prepare →
  raw-value conflict guard → write → reconcile); the existing acceptance check in
  `isLegacyIdentityMigration` (id-only difference) stays the gate for what may be migrated.
* Records that already carry an id are never re-resolved (see Not part of this task).

### O3 — Unpersisted migrations are stable

Rests on: "the same legacy engine therefore receives one id under `engines` and a different one under
each player key".

* With O1/O2 in place, the paths that return a migrated value without persisting it (non-conflict
  write failure, second raw-value conflict, untrusted `engines` evidence) yield the same ids on every
  hydration of the same bytes. No new retry, latch or coordinator is added.

## Decisions and trade-offs

* **Hybrid over a pure cross-key transaction:** a cross-key map built inside one coordinated
  migration needs the three keys written as one unit with a crash boundary, and the player key often
  hydrates in a later session than `engines` (premise: probe 2). Making both sides deterministic
  removes the ordering problem instead of coordinating it.
* **Hybrid over a pure content hash:** hashing each record's fields independently makes the
  `engines` id and the player id agree only while the snapshot is byte-equal to its record; a
  `loaded`/`name`/settings edit made after selection breaks it. Resolving the snapshot against the
  list (tiers 1-3) covers the drift; the deterministic part only has to be stable for unchanged
  bytes, which a positional/list-derived id is, without a hash implementation or a new dependency.
* **"Ties the id to fields a user can edit"** does not apply: ids are derived only for records that
  have none, and any successful write of a key persists the ids, so derivation never re-runs on
  edited bytes.
* The element-level `crypto.randomUUID()` defaults in `src/utils/engines.ts` stay: after this change
  they are unreachable for the three owner keys, and other parse sites (`parsePersistedEngineJson`)
  are outside the mandate.

## Risks / open questions

* Tier 2/3 can pick the wrong record when the user had two `engines` entries on the same executable
  and both name and fields of the snapshot drifted; the result is still an existing engine on the
  same binary, which is strictly better than today's "first remaining engine".
* A derived id coinciding with an id tombstoned by `retire_engine` earlier in the same process only
  arises when a deletion was not persisted; the record then reappears exactly as it does today.

## Not part of this task

* Repairing player snapshots that **already** carry an id absent from the `engines` list (written by
  the per-key random migration since c0016006, or left by a removed engine): they are
  indistinguishable from a deliberately removed engine's selection, which d-20260901-17 retires on
  purpose; re-pointing them by fields would resurrect retired selections.
* The element-level random defaults and `parsePersistedEngineJson` (`src/utils/engines.ts`).
* Startup ownership evidence (`collectOriginalEngineOwnerSnapshot`, `pathOwners.ts`).
* Picker / submission behaviour for a genuinely absent engine (`EnginesSelect.tsx`, `playerConfig.ts`).

## Phases

### Phase 1 — deterministic engine identity and player resolution (O1, O2, O3)

* Files: `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts`, `src/state/atoms.ts` (move/re-export `enginesSchema`,
  deterministic duplicate repair), `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.test.ts`.
* Touches persistence (all three owner keys) and the migration write path; no API contract, no auth.
  Concurrency: no new mechanism; relies on the existing `sequence` and raw-value conflict guard.
* Role: `sensitive` (`src/state/**` is a Sensitive-Path glob).
* Tests (in `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.test.ts`, real schemas and real atoms where the case is
  about hydration order):
  1. pre-id `engines` record plus both player keys holding an equal snapshot: hydrating players before
     `engines`, and `engines` before players, yields one id in all three keys, persisted, and equal
     after a coordinator reset and re-hydration;
  2. `engines` already carrying ids (any value) with the record edited after selection (`loaded`
     toggled; renamed): the pre-id player snapshot resolves to that record's id (tier 1 fails, tier
     2 or 3 applies), including a two-records-same-handle case picking the name match;
  3. `engines` migration write fails (`setItem` throws) and a second-conflict case: two hydrations of
     the same bytes return the same ids, and the player key resolves to them;
  4. no matching record / absent `engines` key: the player gets an id absent from the list, persisted
     and stable across restart; unreadable or invalid `engines` bytes: the player value is returned
     but its raw bytes stay unchanged (no migration write);
  5. duplicate-id repair in `enginesSchema` returns the same repaired ids across two hydrations of
     the same bytes;
  6. the existing migration, conflict and lossy-hydration tests stay green unchanged.
* PROOF: `pnpm test src/state/engineOwnerStorage.test.ts src/state/pathOwners.test.ts src/App.test.tsx src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.test.tsx src/utils/engines.test.ts src/utils/engines.upgrade.test.ts src/components/boards`
  then `pnpm lint` then `pnpm checks:pre-review`.

Final gates per `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md`: `pnpm gates:push -- --frontend` (frontend
coverage ratchet `state-persistence`, build, bundle, e2e container, frontend mutation). No visible
UI change: verify-ui does not apply.

## Decided autonomously

* **Question:** f-20260921-05 — is a pre-id engine identity resolved by a cross-key map built in one coordinated migration, or by a deterministic id derived from the legacy fields?
  **Chosen:** hybrid — ids for the `engines` list are a deterministic function of its stored bytes (position-derived, unique, never colliding with present ids); a player key's pre-id snapshot is resolved against that list (field equality, then handle+name, then handle) and only falls back to a deterministic id absent from the list; untrusted `engines` bytes withhold the player migration write.
  **Rejected:** a cross-key transaction with a crash boundary (the player key often hydrates in a later session than `engines`; needs a new coordination mechanism); a per-record content hash (diverged snapshots would still mismatch; needs a hash implementation or dependency).
  **Reason:** determinism removes ordering, crash and unpersisted-migration instability without a new mechanism, and list resolution covers snapshot drift. Reversal: replace the derivation with any other deterministic function; persisted ids are unaffected.

### plan-r2.md

# Plan: one identity for a pre-id engine across the three engine owner keys (f-20260921-05)

## Goal

A pre-id engine record keeps naming the same engine after migration: the `engines` list and both
game-player owner keys end up carrying the same id for it, whatever order the three keys hydrate in,
whether or not a migration write lands, and across restarts — so the game-player picker and game
submission (which resolve by id since f-20260906-15) find the engine the saved selection names.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20260921-05:

* **Where:** `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts:314-370` (`isLegacyIdentityMigration`, `parseHydratableOwner`), `src/utils/engines.ts:98` (`id: z.string().default(() => crypto.randomUUID())`), `src/state/atoms.ts:194-200` and `480-497` (the three owner keys).
* **Defect:** `engines`, `game-player1-settings` and `game-player2-settings` are hydrated and migrated independently, and the id for a pre-id engine record is minted by the schema's `crypto.randomUUID()` default. The same legacy engine therefore receives one id under `engines` and a different one under each player key. Since f-20260906-15 the selection is validated by id, so after such a migration the saved game-player selection is silently replaced by the first remaining engine (picker) or refused (submission) rather than resolving to the engine it names. Before that validation the mismatch was invisible but equally wrong: the player config carried an id the supervisor did not know.
* **Fix direction:** migration has to reconcile identity across the three owner keys — mint ids once for the `engines` owner and resolve the player owners' legacy records against it by the fields `compareLegacyEngineFields` already compares, rather than defaulting each key independently. The coordinator in d-20260913-01 already serializes the three keys, so the ordering exists; what is missing is a shared identity map. Deriving a stable id from the legacy fields is the alternative and changes what an id means.
* **Open question:** is the pre-id identity resolved by a cross-key map built during one coordinated migration, or by making the minted id a deterministic function of the legacy engine fields? The first keeps ids opaque but needs the three keys migrated in one transaction with a crash boundary; the second makes migration order irrelevant but ties the id to fields a user can edit.
* **Review lens:** persisted-state.

## Threat model and non-goals

Accidental input only: owner values written by earlier versions of this app (records without an
`id`), records that drifted apart after selection (a player snapshot is a copy taken at selection
time; the `engines` record is later edited, toggled or upgraded), migration writes that fail
(quota, native prepare failure) or lose a raw-value conflict, process exit between the writes of
different keys, and two windows of the same app version hydrating concurrently. Out of scope:
deliberately hand-edited `localStorage`, and downgrading to a pre-id app version that writes
id-less records again after migration.

* `src/state/atoms.ts:556-569` — each player owner hydrates through `createEngineOwnerStorage(key, opponentSettingsSchema, defaultPlayerSettings)`; `src/state/opponentSettings.ts:52` its `engine` is a full `localEngineSchema` snapshot, so its id is minted by the same per-parse default.
* `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts:374-384` `parseHydratableOwner` parses each key alone; `:322-372` `isLegacyIdentityMigration` accepts an id-only difference between raw and parsed; `:391-425` `getItem` persists the migration through `migrateEngineOwnerValue` (`:243`, raw-value conflict guard at `:200`), and on a non-conflict failure (`:422`) or the second conflict (`:420`, `allowMigrationRetry=false`) returns the freshly minted value **without persisting it**, so the next launch mints yet another id.
* Probe 2 (`probe-2-r1`): all three owner atoms omit `getOnInit` and hydrate on first subscription; `BoardGame.tsx:167-168` is the only reader of the player owners, while `engines` is subscribed by many screens — there is no hydration order between the keys, and a player key can hydrate in a later session than `engines`.
* Probe 1 (`probe-1-r1`): `src/components/boards/EnginesSelect.tsx:31-37` replaces a selection whose id is absent from the `engines` list with the first remaining local engine (persisted through the player owner); `src/components/boards/playerConfig.ts:39` refuses submission for an absent id; `EnginesSelect.tsx:38-40` refreshes a same-id snapshot from the live record, so a snapshot diverging from its `engines` record is normal (`loaded`/`enabled`/settings/name edits only change the `engines` record); `EnginesSelect.tsx:53` stores the complete `engines` record (with its id) on selection.

* `src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.tsx:77-91` saves the upgraded record through `enginesAtom` (which awaits `engines` hydration first, `src/state/atoms.ts:257-258`) and only afterwards, `:93-111`, hydrates each player owner and applies `upgradeOpponentEngine` (`src/state/opponentSettings.ts:81`, matches by id only); `upgradeEngineFromCatalog` (`src/utils/engines.ts:409-424`) replaces `handle`, `filename` and `name`. ## Approach

The open question is decided as a hybrid that needs no cross-key transaction: **every identity a
migration assigns is a pure function of the stored bytes**, and a player key's pre-id snapshot is
**resolved against the `engines` list** rather than minted. Because both sides are deterministic,
hydration order, a failed write, a crash between two keys' writes, and two concurrent windows all
yield the same ids; no coordinator, lock or transaction is added.

### O1 — Deterministic identity for the `engines` list

Rests on: "the id for a pre-id engine record is minted by the schema's `crypto.randomUUID()`
default"; "mint ids once for the `engines` owner".

* Hydrating the `engines` key assigns every record that has no string `id` an id computed only from
  the decoded list: the same decoded bytes always yield the same ids. Ids are unique within the list
  and never equal any id already present in the raw list. The derivation does not mutate the decoded
  input (the raw-vs-parsed comparison in `isLegacyIdentityMigration` depends on it).
* The duplicate-id repair in `enginesSchema` becomes deterministic the same way (rule 4b, same file
  and same defect class: a re-minted id that is not persisted changes on every launch, so a player
  selection made on the repaired record in that session no longer names it after restart).
* The derived id is an opaque string; its exact format is the executor's choice. Once persisted it is
  never re-derived, so later edits to the record's fields do not change it.
* `enginesSchema` moves out of `src/state/atoms.ts` into `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts` (O2 needs
  it there; `atoms.ts` already imports that module, the reverse would be circular). `atoms.ts` keeps
  exporting it so existing imports stay valid.
* The strict startup inspection (`schemas.engines`, `inspectEngineOwnerValue`,
  `collectOriginalEngineOwnerSnapshot`) is unchanged.

### O2 — A player key's pre-id snapshot resolves to the `engines` record it names

Rests on: "resolve the player owners' legacy records against it"; "rather than resolving to the
engine it names".

* When a player owner (`game-player1-settings` / `game-player2-settings`) hydrates an `engine`
  branch whose `engine` has no string `id`, its id is taken from the `engines` list as O1 would
  hydrate it from the bytes currently stored under `engines` (already-persisted ids, or O1-derived
  ids for a list that is itself still pre-id). It is never minted independently.
* Match rule over the list's local engines. The pre-id identity of an engine entry is its
  executable `handle.id` together with its `name` (pre-id builds keyed engines by name, `5b5d006a`
  in `.claude/rules/persisted-state.md`). Candidates are the local engines with the snapshot's
  `handle.id` and `name`. Exactly one candidate → that record. Several candidates → the first, in
  list order, that equals the snapshot on every field except `id` (`compareLegacyEngineFields`;
  field-identical entries produce identical game configurations); if none of them is field-equal,
  the snapshot is **unresolved**. No candidate → unresolved. A shared executable alone never
  resolves a snapshot, so an entry is never aliased to a different entry on the same binary
  (plan review r1, I2). Field drift that keeps handle and name (`loaded`, `enabled`, settings,
  premise `EnginesSelect.tsx:38-40`) still resolves.
* Unresolved (the engine was removed, renamed in a pre-id build, or the `engines` key is absent): the
  snapshot gets a deterministic id that is not present in the `engines` list, so the existing
  picker/submission "absent engine" handling applies unchanged and stays stable across launches.
* Failure semantics: if the `engines` bytes cannot be read, decoded, or hydrated (the same
  conditions under which the `engines` owner itself falls back to its initial value), the resolution
  has no trusted evidence — the player value is returned for display but its migration is **not
  persisted**, so no guessed id is written. Known limit, not changed here: a mounted game-player
  picker then sees the fallback empty list and replaces the selection itself — pre-existing for every
  saved selection, filed as its own finding (inbox `20261007-044921-3928298-1791341361022270652-6.md`,
  plan review r1, I4).
* The resolved id is persisted through the existing `migrateEngineOwnerValue` route (prepare →
  raw-value conflict guard → write → reconcile); the existing acceptance check in
  `isLegacyIdentityMigration` (id-only difference) stays the gate for what may be migrated.
* Records that already carry an id are never re-resolved (see Not part of this task).

### O4 — Hydrating `engines` settles pre-id player snapshots before any `engines` write

Rests on: "migration has to reconcile identity across the three owner keys — mint ids once for the
`engines` owner and resolve the player owners' legacy records against it".

* When the `engines` owner hydrates from trusted bytes, its `getItem` — after its own migration
  attempt and before it resolves — resolves every player key whose stored bytes hold a pre-id
  `engine` snapshot against the hydrated list (the O2 rule, same function) and persists it through
  the same `migrateEngineOwnerValue` route with that key's raw-value conflict guard. Every
  `enginesAtom` write awaits that hydration (`src/state/atoms.ts:257-258`), so an `engines` write
  that changes matching evidence — a catalog upgrade replacing `handle`/`name`, a rename, a removal —
  can only land after the pre-id player snapshots were resolved against the unchanged list
  (plan review r1, I1).
* A player key that is absent, already carries an id, or is not a valid owner value is left alone.
  A failed player write (quota, prepare failure, lost raw-value conflict) is reported through the
  existing persistence-error route and never fails or delays the `engines` hydration's own result
  beyond that attempt; the player key stays pre-id and is resolved again by the next hydration of
  either key (known limit: if an `engines` write changed that record's handle and name in between, it
  resolves as unresolved).
* The player owner's own hydration (O2) stays: it covers a player key hydrating before `engines`
  in a session. Both paths call the one resolver; a concurrent pair converges through the existing
  raw-value conflict guard because both compute the same value.

### O3 — Unpersisted migrations are stable

Rests on: "the same legacy engine therefore receives one id under `engines` and a different one under
each player key".

* With O1/O2 in place, the paths that return a migrated value without persisting it (non-conflict
  write failure, second raw-value conflict, untrusted `engines` evidence) yield the same ids on every
  hydration of the same bytes. No new retry, latch or coordinator is added.

## Decisions and trade-offs

* **Hybrid over a pure cross-key transaction:** a cross-key map built inside one coordinated
  migration needs the three keys written as one unit with a crash boundary, and the player key often
  hydrates in a later session than `engines` (premise: probe 2). Making both sides deterministic
  removes the ordering problem instead of coordinating it.
* **Hybrid over a pure content hash:** hashing each record's fields independently makes the
  `engines` id and the player id agree only while the snapshot is byte-equal to its record; a
  `loaded`/settings edit made after selection breaks it. Resolving the snapshot against the
  list by handle + name covers the drift; the deterministic part only has to be stable for unchanged
  bytes, which a positional/list-derived id is, without a hash implementation or a new dependency.
* **"Ties the id to fields a user can edit"** does not apply: ids are derived only for records that
  have none, and any successful write of a key persists the ids, so derivation never re-runs on
  edited bytes.
* The element-level `crypto.randomUUID()` defaults in `src/utils/engines.ts` stay: after this change
  they are unreachable for the three owner keys, and other parse sites (`parsePersistedEngineJson`)
  are outside the mandate.

## Risks / open questions

* A pre-id selection whose engine was renamed while still pre-id stays unresolved (handle + name is
  the identity); the picker then shows its existing "absent engine" behaviour, as today.
* A derived id coinciding with an id tombstoned by `retire_engine` earlier in the same process only
  arises when a deletion was not persisted; the record then reappears exactly as it does today.

## Not part of this task

* Repairing player snapshots that **already** carry an id absent from the `engines` list (written by
  the per-key random migration since c0016006, or left by a removed engine): they are
  indistinguishable from a deliberately removed engine's selection, which d-20260901-17 retires on
  purpose; re-pointing them by fields would resurrect retired selections.
* The element-level random defaults and `parsePersistedEngineJson` (`src/utils/engines.ts`).
* Startup ownership evidence (`collectOriginalEngineOwnerSnapshot`, `pathOwners.ts`).
* Picker / submission behaviour for a genuinely absent engine (`EnginesSelect.tsx`, `playerConfig.ts`).

## Phases

### Phase 1 — deterministic engine identity and player resolution (O1, O2, O3, O4)

* Files: `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts`, `src/state/atoms.ts` (move/re-export `enginesSchema`,
  deterministic duplicate repair), `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.test.ts`,
  `src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.test.tsx` (test 7 only).
* Touches persistence (all three owner keys) and the migration write path; no API contract, no auth.
  Concurrency: no new mechanism; relies on the existing `sequence` and raw-value conflict guard.
* Role: `sensitive` (`src/state/**` is a Sensitive-Path glob).
* Tests (in `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.test.ts`, real schemas and real atoms where the case is
  about hydration order):
  1. pre-id `engines` record plus both player keys holding an equal snapshot: hydrating players before
     `engines`, and `engines` before players, yields one id in all three keys, persisted, and equal
     after a coordinator reset and re-hydration;
  2. `engines` already carrying ids (any value) with the record's `loaded` flag or settings changed
     after selection: the pre-id player snapshot resolves to that record's id; two entries sharing
     handle and name where the snapshot is field-equal to the second resolve to the second; the same
     pair with the snapshot equal to neither stays unresolved; an entry sharing only the handle (the
     selected entry removed, a sibling on the same binary left) stays unresolved;
  3. `engines` migration write fails (`setItem` throws) while a player key's migration persists, and
     a second-conflict case: after `vi.resetModules()`, a coordinator reset and fresh store/storage
     instances (a fresh runtime) with the `engines` bytes still pre-id, the re-hydrated `engines` id
     equals the persisted player id;
  4. no matching record / absent `engines` key: the player gets an id absent from the list, persisted
     and stable across restart; unreadable or invalid `engines` bytes: the player value is returned
     but its raw bytes stay unchanged (no migration write);
  5. duplicate-id repair in `enginesSchema` returns the same repaired ids across two hydrations of
     the same bytes;
  6. the existing migration, conflict and lossy-hydration tests stay green unchanged;
  7. O4: hydrating only `engines` persists the resolved id into both pre-id player keys; a failed
     player write leaves the `engines` hydration result intact; and in
     `src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.test.tsx`, a pre-id player snapshot of the upgraded
     engine that was never hydrated before the upgrade ends with the engine's id and the upgraded
     binary (`upgradeOpponentEngine` applied).
* PROOF: `pnpm test src/state/engineOwnerStorage.test.ts src/state/pathOwners.test.ts src/App.test.tsx src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.test.tsx src/utils/engines.test.ts src/utils/engines.upgrade.test.ts src/components/boards`
  then `pnpm lint` then `pnpm checks:pre-review`.

Final gates per `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md`: `pnpm gates:push -- --frontend` (frontend
coverage ratchet `state-persistence`, build, bundle, e2e container, frontend mutation). No visible
UI change: verify-ui does not apply.

## Decided autonomously

* **Question:** f-20260921-05 — is a pre-id engine identity resolved by a cross-key map built in one coordinated migration, or by a deterministic id derived from the legacy fields?
  **Chosen:** hybrid — ids for the `engines` list are a deterministic function of its stored bytes (position-derived, unique, never colliding with present ids); a player key's pre-id snapshot is resolved against that list by handle + name (field equality breaks a tie, otherwise unresolved) and only falls back to a deterministic id absent from the list; hydrating `engines` settles pre-id player snapshots before any `engines` write; untrusted `engines` bytes withhold the player migration write.
  **Rejected:** a cross-key transaction with a crash boundary (the player key often hydrates in a later session than `engines`; needs a new coordination mechanism); a per-record content hash (diverged snapshots would still mismatch; needs a hash implementation or dependency).
  **Reason:** determinism removes ordering, crash and unpersisted-migration instability without a new mechanism, and list resolution covers snapshot drift. Reversal: replace the derivation with any other deterministic function; persisted ids are unaffected.

slot's `run/evidence/`); this record carries stable issue IDs, witnesses (lens, round, 1-based raw
finding ordinal), dispositions and closure. Executor: Codex (OpenAI high tier) for every lens; the
same orchestrator context wrote the plan and arbitrates.

### plan-r3.md

# Plan: one identity for a pre-id engine across the three engine owner keys (f-20260921-05)

## Goal

A pre-id engine record keeps naming the same engine after migration: the `engines` list and both
game-player owner keys end up carrying the same id for it, whatever order the three keys hydrate in,
whether or not a migration write lands, and across restarts — so the game-player picker and game
submission (which resolve by id since f-20260906-15) find the engine the saved selection names.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20260921-05:

* **Where:** `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts:314-370` (`isLegacyIdentityMigration`, `parseHydratableOwner`), `src/utils/engines.ts:98` (`id: z.string().default(() => crypto.randomUUID())`), `src/state/atoms.ts:194-200` and `480-497` (the three owner keys).
* **Defect:** `engines`, `game-player1-settings` and `game-player2-settings` are hydrated and migrated independently, and the id for a pre-id engine record is minted by the schema's `crypto.randomUUID()` default. The same legacy engine therefore receives one id under `engines` and a different one under each player key. Since f-20260906-15 the selection is validated by id, so after such a migration the saved game-player selection is silently replaced by the first remaining engine (picker) or refused (submission) rather than resolving to the engine it names. Before that validation the mismatch was invisible but equally wrong: the player config carried an id the supervisor did not know.
* **Fix direction:** migration has to reconcile identity across the three owner keys — mint ids once for the `engines` owner and resolve the player owners' legacy records against it by the fields `compareLegacyEngineFields` already compares, rather than defaulting each key independently. The coordinator in d-20260913-01 already serializes the three keys, so the ordering exists; what is missing is a shared identity map. Deriving a stable id from the legacy fields is the alternative and changes what an id means.
* **Open question:** is the pre-id identity resolved by a cross-key map built during one coordinated migration, or by making the minted id a deterministic function of the legacy engine fields? The first keeps ids opaque but needs the three keys migrated in one transaction with a crash boundary; the second makes migration order irrelevant but ties the id to fields a user can edit.
* **Review lens:** persisted-state.

## Threat model and non-goals

Accidental input only: owner values written by earlier versions of this app (records without an
`id`), records that drifted apart after selection (a player snapshot is a copy taken at selection
time; the `engines` record is later edited, toggled or upgraded), migration writes that fail
(quota, native prepare failure) or lose a raw-value conflict, process exit between the writes of
different keys, and two windows of the same app version hydrating concurrently. Out of scope:
deliberately hand-edited `localStorage`, and downgrading to a pre-id app version that writes
id-less records again after migration.

## Traced premises

* `src/utils/engines.ts:111` and `:231` — `localEngineSchema` / `remoteEngineSchema` default a missing `id` to `crypto.randomUUID()`; every parse of the same id-less bytes mints a different id.
* `src/state/atoms.ts:221-230` — `enginesSchema` re-mints a duplicate id with `crypto.randomUUID()`; `:233-237` the `engines` owner hydrates through `createEngineOwnerStorage("engines", enginesSchema, [])`.
* `src/state/atoms.ts:556-569` — each player owner hydrates through `createEngineOwnerStorage(key, opponentSettingsSchema, defaultPlayerSettings)`; `src/state/opponentSettings.ts:52` its `engine` is a full `localEngineSchema` snapshot, so its id is minted by the same per-parse default.
* `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts:374-384` `parseHydratableOwner` parses each key alone; `:322-372` `isLegacyIdentityMigration` accepts an id-only difference between raw and parsed; `:391-425` `getItem` persists the migration through `migrateEngineOwnerValue` (`:243`, raw-value conflict guard at `:200`), and on a non-conflict failure (`:422`) or the second conflict (`:420`, `allowMigrationRetry=false`) returns the freshly minted value **without persisting it**, so the next launch mints yet another id.
* Probe 2 (`probe-2-r1`): all three owner atoms omit `getOnInit` and hydrate on first subscription; `BoardGame.tsx:167-168` is the only reader of the player owners, while `engines` is subscribed by many screens — there is no hydration order between the keys, and a player key can hydrate in a later session than `engines`.
* Probe 1 (`probe-1-r1`): `src/components/boards/EnginesSelect.tsx:31-37` replaces a selection whose id is absent from the `engines` list with the first remaining local engine (persisted through the player owner); `src/components/boards/playerConfig.ts:39` refuses submission for an absent id; `EnginesSelect.tsx:38-40` refreshes a same-id snapshot from the live record, so a snapshot diverging from its `engines` record is normal (`loaded`/`enabled`/settings/name edits only change the `engines` record); `EnginesSelect.tsx:53` stores the complete `engines` record (with its id) on selection.
* The serialization the finding attributes to d-20260913-01 is in fact the module-level `sequence` chain in `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts:158-234`; d-20260913-01 is the database A2 operation matrix and does not govern this cluster.
* `src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.tsx:77-91` saves the upgraded record through `enginesAtom` (which awaits `engines` hydration first, `src/state/atoms.ts:257-258`) and only afterwards, `:93-111`, hydrates each player owner and applies `upgradeOpponentEngine` (`src/state/opponentSettings.ts:81`, matches by id only); `upgradeEngineFromCatalog` (`src/utils/engines.ts:409-424`) replaces `handle`, `filename` and `name`. A pre-id player snapshot first hydrated at `:104` therefore sees a record whose handle and name no longer match it (plan review r1, I1).
* `src-tauri/src/engine/process.rs:919`, `:1939` — native engine ids are plain `String`s; `retire_engine` tombstones an id for the process lifetime (d-20260901-17). No production schema validates an engine id's shape (`git grep '\.uuid()' src` hits only tab and workspace ids).

## Approach

The open question is decided without a cross-key transaction: **the id a migration assigns to a
pre-id engine is a deterministic digest of that engine's pre-id identity** — its executable
`handle.id` together with its `name` (pre-id builds keyed engines by name, `5b5d006a` in
`.claude/rules/persisted-state.md`). The `engines` list and a player snapshot taken from it carry the
same pre-id identity, so each key computes the same id **from its own bytes**: hydration order, a
failed or lost write on either key, a crash between two keys' writes, two concurrent windows, and a
later edit that changes the record's handle or name (catalog upgrade, rename) all leave the two ids
equal. Matching against the `engines` list survives only for data the defective migration already
rewrote with random ids (O2, O4).

### O1 — Deterministic identity for the `engines` list

Rests on: "the id for a pre-id engine record is minted by the schema's `crypto.randomUUID()`
default"; "mint ids once for the `engines` owner".

* Hydrating the `engines` key assigns every record that has no string `id` an id computed only from
  the decoded list; the same decoded bytes always yield the same ids, and the derivation does not
  mutate the decoded input (the raw-vs-parsed comparison in `isLegacyIdentityMigration` depends on
  it).
* A **local** id-less record whose (`handle.id`, `name`) pair is unique among the list's local
  records gets the **identity id** `D(handle.id, name)`. Every other id-less record — a remote
  (`chessdb`/`lichess`) record, or a member of a group of local records sharing one (`handle.id`,
  `name`) — gets a **positional id** (a deterministic function of its list position) from a
  namespace disjoint from identity ids, so no snapshot can ever compute it (plan review r1/r2, I2:
  an ambiguous group is never resolvable by a snapshot).
* `D` is a bounded, opaque, synchronous digest of the pair (no new dependency, does not embed the
  name; a 64-bit-or-wider non-cryptographic digest suffices — inputs are not adversarial, threat
  model). Every assigned id is unique within the list and never equals an id already present in the
  raw list; if an identity id would collide, that record is treated as ambiguous (positional id).
* The duplicate-id repair in `enginesSchema` becomes deterministic as well, with positional ids (rule
  4b, same file and defect class: a re-minted id that is not persisted changes on every launch).
* Once persisted an id is never re-derived; later edits to a record's fields do not change it.
* `enginesSchema` moves out of `src/state/atoms.ts` into `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts` (O2 and
  O4 need it there; `atoms.ts` already imports that module, the reverse would be circular).
  `atoms.ts` keeps exporting it so existing imports stay valid.
* The strict startup inspection (`schemas.engines`, `inspectEngineOwnerValue`,
  `collectOriginalEngineOwnerSnapshot`) is unchanged.

### O2 — A player key's pre-id snapshot resolves to the `engines` record it names

Rests on: "resolve the player owners' legacy records against it"; "rather than resolving to the
engine it names".

* When a player owner (`game-player1-settings` / `game-player2-settings`) hydrates an `engine`
  branch whose `engine` has no string `id`, the snapshot's id is `D(snapshot.handle.id,
  snapshot.name)` — the identity id O1 gives its `engines` record. It is never minted independently.
  This needs no read of the `engines` key and is the result whenever the `engines` list holds a
  record with that id, or holds none (removed, ambiguous group, renamed while still pre-id, key
  absent) — then the id is absent from the list and the existing picker/submission "absent engine"
  handling applies, stable across launches.
* **Random-era fallback** (premise: `engineOwnerStorage.ts:391-425` has persisted random ids for
  `engines` since c0016006, while a player key may still be pre-id): if the trusted `engines` list
  holds no record with id `D`, and holds **exactly one** local record with the snapshot's
  (`handle.id`, `name`) whose id is neither an identity id nor a positional id (i.e. it was minted
  elsewhere), the snapshot takes that record's id. Any other count, or an O1-derived id, leaves `D`
  (unresolved). Field drift that keeps handle and name (`loaded`, `enabled`, settings, premise
  `EnginesSelect.tsx:38-40`) does not affect either rule. Known limit: the fallback also binds a
  selection to an entry the user removed and re-added with the same binary and name.
* Failure semantics: only the fallback reads the `engines` key. If those bytes cannot be read,
  decoded, or hydrated (the conditions under which the `engines` owner itself falls back to its
  initial value), the result `D` is returned for display but its migration is **not persisted**, so
  no guessed id is written. Known limit, not changed here: a mounted game-player picker then sees the
  fallback empty list and replaces the selection itself — pre-existing for every saved selection,
  filed as its own finding (inbox `20261007-044921-3928298-1791341361022270652-6.md`, plan review r1,
  I4).
* The result is persisted through the existing `migrateEngineOwnerValue` route (prepare →
  raw-value conflict guard → write → reconcile); the existing acceptance check in
  `isLegacyIdentityMigration` (id-only difference) stays the gate for what may be migrated.
* Records that already carry an id are never re-resolved (see Not part of this task).

### O4 — Hydrating `engines` settles pre-id player snapshots before any `engines` write

Rests on: "migration has to reconcile identity across the three owner keys — mint ids once for the
`engines` owner and resolve the player owners' legacy records against it".

* Needed only for the random-era fallback: its evidence (handle and name of the current record) is
  changed by a catalog upgrade, a rename or a removal, while `D` is not (plan review r1, I1).
* When the `engines` owner hydrates from trusted bytes, its `getItem` — after its own migration
  attempt and before it resolves — runs the O2 resolution (same function) for every player key whose
  stored bytes hold a pre-id `engine` snapshot, and persists it through the same
  `migrateEngineOwnerValue` route with that key's raw-value conflict guard. Every `enginesAtom` write
  awaits that hydration (`src/state/atoms.ts:257-258`), so an evidence-changing `engines` write lands
  after the settle attempt.
* A player key that is absent, already carries an id, or is not a valid owner value is left alone. A
  failed player write is reported through the existing persistence-error route and never fails the
  `engines` hydration's own result; the player key stays pre-id and is settled again by the next
  hydration of either key. Known limit: random-era data only — if that write failed and an
  evidence-changing `engines` write follows before any successful settle, the snapshot stays
  unresolved (pre-id-era data is unaffected because `D` does not depend on the list).
* The player owner's own hydration (O2) stays: it covers a player key hydrating before `engines` in
  a session. A concurrent pair converges through the existing raw-value conflict guard because both
  compute the same value.

### O3 — Unpersisted migrations are stable

Rests on: "the same legacy engine therefore receives one id under `engines` and a different one under
each player key".

* With O1/O2 in place, the paths that return a migrated value without persisting it (non-conflict
  write failure, second raw-value conflict, untrusted `engines` evidence) yield the same ids on every
  hydration of the same bytes. No new retry, latch or coordinator is added.

## Decisions and trade-offs

* **Identity digest over a cross-key transaction:** a map built inside one coordinated migration
  needs the three keys written as one unit with a crash boundary, and the player key often hydrates
  in a later session than `engines` (premise: probe 2); a later upgrade or rename then destroys the
  evidence a deferred match needs (r1/r2, I1). Deriving the id from the pre-id identity lets each
  key compute it from its own bytes.
* **Handle + name, not every field:** a snapshot legitimately diverges from its record in `loaded`,
  `enabled` and settings; hashing all fields would split them. Handle + name was the pre-id identity.
* **Ambiguity is never resolved:** a group sharing handle and name gets positional ids no snapshot
  can compute; field equality is not used to choose between entries (r2, I2).
* **"Ties the id to fields a user can edit"** does not apply: ids are derived only for records that
  have none, from the pre-id handle and name, and any successful write of a key persists them, so
  derivation never re-runs on edited bytes.
* The element-level `crypto.randomUUID()` defaults in `src/utils/engines.ts` stay: after this change
  they are unreachable for the three owner keys, and other parse sites (`parsePersistedEngineJson`)
  are outside the mandate.

## Risks / open questions

* A pre-id selection whose engine was renamed, or removed and replaced by another with the same
  binary and name, while still pre-id, resolves by the pre-id identity: the former stays unresolved,
  the latter binds to the replacement. Both match how pre-id builds identified engines.
* A derived id coinciding with an id tombstoned by `retire_engine` earlier in the same process only
  arises when a deletion was not persisted; the record then reappears exactly as it does today.
* The random-era fallback's double-failure limit (O4).

## Not part of this task

* Repairing player snapshots that **already** carry an id absent from the `engines` list (written by
  the per-key random migration since c0016006, or left by a removed engine): they are
  indistinguishable from a deliberately removed engine's selection, which d-20260901-17 retires on
  purpose; re-pointing them by fields would resurrect retired selections.
* The element-level random defaults and `parsePersistedEngineJson` (`src/utils/engines.ts`).
* Startup ownership evidence (`collectOriginalEngineOwnerSnapshot`, `pathOwners.ts`).
* Picker / submission behaviour for a genuinely absent engine (`EnginesSelect.tsx`, `playerConfig.ts`).

## Phases

### Phase 1 — deterministic engine identity and player resolution (O1, O2, O3, O4)

* Files: `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.ts`, `src/state/atoms.ts` (move/re-export `enginesSchema`,
  deterministic duplicate repair), `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.test.ts`,
  `src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.test.tsx` (test 7 only).
* Touches persistence (all three owner keys) and the migration write path; no API contract, no auth.
  Concurrency: no new mechanism; relies on the existing `sequence` and raw-value conflict guard.
* Role: `sensitive` (`src/state/**` is a Sensitive-Path glob).
* Tests (in `src/state/engineOwnerStorage.test.ts`, real schemas and real atoms where the case is
  about hydration order):
  1. pre-id `engines` record plus both player keys holding a snapshot of it (with `loaded`/settings
     drift on the `engines` side): hydrating players before `engines`, and `engines` before players,
     yields one id in all three keys, persisted, and equal after a coordinator reset and re-hydration;
  2. ambiguity and the random-era fallback: a pre-id group of two local records sharing handle and
     name, the snapshot equal to one of them → the player id is absent from the hydrated list (also
     after one member is removed); random-era list (ids minted elsewhere) with exactly one
     handle+name match → that id; two such matches → unresolved; the selected entry removed and a
     sibling on the same binary with another name left → unresolved;
  3. failure and restart: the `engines` migration write fails (`setItem` throws) while a player key's
     migration persists, and a second-conflict case; then after `vi.resetModules()`, a coordinator
     reset and fresh store/storage instances (a fresh runtime) with the `engines` bytes still pre-id,
     the re-hydrated `engines` id equals the persisted player id; and the reverse (player write fails,
     `engines` persists, then the record's handle and name change) — after a fresh runtime the player
     id still equals the record's id;
  4. untrusted `engines` bytes (unreadable or invalid) with a random-era-shaped player: the player
     value is returned but its raw bytes stay unchanged (no migration write); absent `engines` key:
     the player id is persisted and stable across restart;
  5. duplicate-id repair in `enginesSchema` returns the same repaired ids across two hydrations of
     the same bytes, and a remote id-less record gets a deterministic positional id;
  6. the existing migration, conflict and lossy-hydration tests stay green unchanged;
  7. O4: random-era list, hydrating only `engines` persists the fallback id into both pre-id player
     keys; a failed player write leaves the `engines` hydration result intact; and in
     `src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.test.tsx`, a pre-id player snapshot of the upgraded
     engine that was never hydrated before the upgrade ends with the engine's id and the upgraded
     binary (`upgradeOpponentEngine` applied) — once for a pre-id `engines` list and once for a
     random-era list.
* PROOF: `pnpm test src/state/engineOwnerStorage.test.ts src/state/pathOwners.test.ts src/App.test.tsx src/components/engines/UpgradeEngineModal.test.tsx src/utils/engines.test.ts src/utils/engines.upgrade.test.ts src/components/boards`
  then `pnpm lint` then `pnpm checks:pre-review`.

Final gates per `.claude/skills/push/SKILL.md`: `pnpm gates:push -- --frontend` (frontend
coverage ratchet `state-persistence`, build, bundle, e2e container, frontend mutation). No visible
UI change: verify-ui does not apply.

## Decided autonomously

* **Question:** f-20260921-05 — is a pre-id engine identity resolved by a cross-key map built in one coordinated migration, or by a deterministic id derived from the legacy fields?
  **Chosen:** identity digest — a pre-id local engine whose handle + name is unique in the `engines` list gets `D(handle.id, name)`, and a pre-id player snapshot gets `D` of its own handle + name, so both keys agree from their own bytes; ambiguous groups and remote records get positional ids no snapshot can compute; data already migrated with random ids is matched by a unique handle + name (random-era fallback), settled when `engines` hydrates; untrusted `engines` bytes withhold that fallback's write.
  **Rejected:** a cross-key transaction with a crash boundary (the player key often hydrates in a later session than `engines`, and a later upgrade or rename destroys the evidence a deferred match needs); a digest over every field (snapshot drift in `loaded`/settings would split ids); resolving ambiguity by field equality (cannot tell entries apart, plan review I2).
  **Reason:** a digest of the pre-id identity lets each key compute the shared id from its own bytes, independent of order, write failures and later edits. Reversal: change `D` before any release; persisted ids are unaffected afterwards.

## Reviews

Append-only. Raw lens reports are kept verbatim under `$RUN_TMP/lens-<lens>-r<N>.txt` (mirrored to the
slot's `run/evidence/`); this record carries stable issue IDs, witnesses (lens, round, 1-based raw
finding ordinal), dispositions and closure. Executor: Codex (OpenAI high tier) for every lens; the
same orchestrator context wrote the plan and arbitrates.

Raw verdicts: review-plan REVISE (1 finding) · review-minimalism APPROVED (0) · review-correctness
